# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1980

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Rūpānuga dāsa

Vyāsa-pūjā means the offering of homage to one who has been authorized to speak from the seat first occupied by Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the original compiler of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, the essence of Vedic knowledge. Vyāsadeva first received this transcendental knowledge from Nārada Muni, who received it from Lord Brahmā. Vyāsadeva in turn passed it down through the disciplic succession to Śrīla Prabhupāda, who never altered a syllable. Śrīla Prabhupāda once explained, “The spiritual master is the representative of Vyāsa because he is delivering that same knowledge that is coming down by disciplic succession from Vyāsa. Because he delivers that knowledge without change, he is offered respects one day out of the year on his appearance day as the representative of Vyāsadeva, and this is called Vyāsa-pūjā.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda gives the example of the postman, who may be imperfect in so many ways, but who, when he does his business perfectly – delivering the mail without alteration – is perfect. In other words, the spiritual master’s claim is not that he himself is perfect, but that he doesn’t cheat anyone. The spiritual master never thinks of himself as sitting comfortably above everyone else and accepting all honors and contributions for himself, because he is not attached to the fruits of his work – the disciples, the opulence, the absolute obedience. Rather, he humbly accepts all these things on behalf of Lord Kṛṣṇa and the disciplic succession. In a Vyāsa-pūjā address, Śrīla Prabhupāda described the sum and substance of Vyāsa-pūjā: “The honor and homage to the spiritual master are needed just to teach the student how to offer them to God.”

Offering Vyāsa-pūjā to Śrīla Prabhupāda has special meaning. Although he would never allow such praise in his personal presence, history will compare Śrīla Prabhupāda’s contribution in the disciplic succession to that of the influence of the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana. The stature of a Vaiṣṇava is judged by the books, devotees and spiritual institutions he has produced. The number of devotees and the ISKCON branch of the Caitanya tree that Śrīla Prabhupāda has created, the number of literatures he has written and translated, and the number of temples, schools and farm projects he has founded, when taken together, have made such an impact upon the modern world that the course of human civilization is being redirected toward the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Such an achievement is practically inconceivable and certainly worthy of eternal praise.

Therefore, all the members of ISKCON must always pay homage to Śrīla Prabhupāda. Prabhu means “master” and pāda means “lotus feet”; so Prabhupāda means “that master at whose feet everyone offers respects.” The members of ISKCON must seriously emulate the example of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s life and carefully follow his teachings, thus preserving the purity of the ISKCON Gauḍīya-Vaiṣṇava-sampradāya and assuring that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā will be celebrated with great devotion by all the devotees for thousands of years to come.

Rūpānuga dāsa

## Introduction

Devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead is the greatest science. It is not a sentimental, religious process invented at a certain time of place. People have followed the same regulative principles of devotional service, as prescribed in the Vedic scriptures, since time immemorial. It is an ageless process for attaining our eternal, blissful life in the spiritual world.

A hint of Kṛṣṇa’s scientific arrangements is revealed by perceiving material nature’s delicate balance, as well as the exact and consistent laws and principles under which it works. Śrī Iśopaniṣad states,

*oṁ pūrṇam adaḥ pūrṇam idaṁ*  
*pūrṇāt pūrṇam udacyate*

**“The Personality of Godhead is perfect and complete, and because He is completely perfect, all emanations from Him, such as this phenomenal world, are perfectly equipped as a complete whole.”** ***\[*****Īśo Invocation\]**

Not only is material nature logical and consistent, but so is devotional service, both being manifest from the Supreme Personality of Godhead. The Lord cannot be realized by any arbitrary process: the method for understanding Him cannot be whimsical either in form or content. A sentimental show of emotion will not reveal Him to anyone.

As various plants can grow in an agricultural field if properly cared for, similarly, the *bhakti-latā*, the creeper of devotion to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, must be cultivated in a precise way. That way is enunciated by the Supreme Lord Himself: *dharmaṁ tu sākṣāt-bhagavat-praṇītam.* The cultivation of love of God through devotional service is the essence of all scriptures of the world. The scientific practice of pure devotion to Kṛṣṇa can develop within its followers their natural loving sentiment, which attracts the Supreme Lord.

Following the path charted by the Lord and His bona fide representatives helps one to progress gradually to the goal. The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.2.12) states,

*tac chraddadhānā munayo*  
*jñāna-vairāgya-yuktayā*  
*paśyanti ātmani cātmānaṁ*  
*bhaktyā śruta-gṛhītayā*

**“The seriously inquisitive student of sage, well equipped with knowledge and detachment, realizes the Absolute Truth by rendering devotional service in terms of what he has heard from the** ***Vedānta-śruti.*****”**

Those activities alleged as devotional service which do not conform with the regulative principles of *bhakti-yoga* have been described by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī as a disturbance to human society. In the *Brahma-yāmala* it is stated, “If someone wants to pose himself as a great devotee without following the authorities or the revealed scriptures, then his activities will never help him to make progress in devotional service. Instead, he will simply create disturbances for the sincere students of devotional service.”

Our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda has very appropriately been given the name Bhaktivedanta. *Bhakti* means “devotion to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa,” and vedānta means “the essence of all knowledge.” To fulfill their desires for sense gratification and mental speculation, foolish people propound nonsensical philosophies in the name of *bhakti* and Vedānta, which diverts people from the actual path of spiritual perfection. Śrīla Prabhupāda has, in contrast, clearly delineated in his lectures and writings the actual path of *bhakti*, with extensive reference to Vedānta. By his personal example he has taught how to practically apply these teachings. Contemporary human society has been shown that religion can be both scientific and reasonable.

Śrīla Prabhupāda has fearlessly cut back all the overgrowth that has hidden the path leading back to Godhead. He has shown by the strength of his perfect purity and Kṛṣṇa consciousness how one can become completely detached from the illusory attractions of the material nature. Let us, his disciples and granddisciples, carefully follow in his sacred footsteps and return back to Godhead. Let us bring with us as many conditional souls as will agree to listen to the message we have received from Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lotus mouth. This is our only responsibility, our attempt at repaying him for the matchless gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Jagadīśa dāsa

**Ohe! Śrīla Prabhupāda**

**(1)**

*ohe!*  
*śrīla prabhupāda taba nāma gāi*  
*e sevā nitya karibo*  
*kabe āmi pāibo tomāra āśirbāda*  
*kabe icchā hīna hoiba?*

**(2)**

*je pāścātya deśe bhakti bitarila*  
*kemane sāhājya karibo?*  
*gaurāṅgera saṅge nabadvīpe nāche*  
*jagate kare nām bāno*

**(3)**

*āmāra śarīra, mana, byartha hoi*  
*upabhoga kori tabu-o*  
*mora deha, buddhi*  
*vākya, tamār sebāi*  
*sabe niyukta karibo*

**(4)**

*nityānandera haite avatār prasādera*  
*uttama kṛṣṇera sebaka*  
*tomāra pracārer śakati jagater*  
*ābaraṇa, bidāraka*

**(5)**

*bahu grantha lekhe barṇanā gaurāṅger*  
*sarva-lok jana jurāite*  
*bitaraṇ e jñāna kori, duḥkhī jane*  
*mora prāṇ saṁpūrṇa haite*

(1) O Śrīla Prabhupāda! I am singing your name. This service I will perform eternally. When will I receive your blessings? And when I become free from all of my own sinful desires?

(2) He who distributed devotional service to Kṛṣṇa to the Western countries – how can I assist him? He is dancing in the association of Lord Gaurāṅga in Navadvīpa, and they are inundating the universe with the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.

(3) My body and my mind are simply useless. Nevertheless, I am still trying to enjoy them. My body, intelligence, and words – please engage them in your service.

(4) You are the mercy incarnation of Nityānanda Prabhu, and you are the topmost servant of Lord Kṛṣṇa. The power of your preaching is piercing the covering of the universe.

(5) You are writing many books describing the glories of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Gaurāṅga, and you are giving soothing relief to the entire human society. Let me simply distribute this knowledge to those who are suffering and unhappy. Then my life will be perfect.

Parasatya dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the G.B.C.

## Ādi Keśava Swami

Many days as I watch the sun rise I reflect on the words *kṛṣṇa-sūrya sama, māyā haya andhakāra.* I think of Lord Govinda and His sunlike grandeur reflected in the unlimitedly faceted personality composed of *cintāmaṇi* that is Śrīla Prabhupāda. Like the gems illuminating the royal palaces of the Dvāpara-yuga, this *ācāryadeva-śiromaṇi* illuminates the darkened corridors of my heart with his endlessly expanding features of pure devotion and destroys the desire to pursue the prowling of the mundane mind.

Sometimes he appears like a sapphire, a cold cerulean blue, with a hard and demanding quality of immeasurable intensity. At others he is like a ruby, glowing with warmth and compassion, full of the loving assurance of eternal fatherhood. In other moments there appears to be an emeraldlike quality, full of freshness and radiance, as if born out of some original paradise. And always he manifests the glittering brilliance of diamond, sparkling with infinite hues of good humor but never reflecting the mixed substance of the modes of material nature.

One could peer endlessly into the heart of this gem and never lose one’s sense of awe and fascination. What timeless past and history does he hold? How does he become the most prized possession for those who are bearing him within the heart and serving him in a mood of timeless devotion?

What promise there is for the future within this beautiful jewel, set at the crest of a diadem composed of a galaxy of transcendental gems reflecting the brilliance of Lord Govinda’s lotus feet! Within this jewel one will find knowledge of the source of the universal manifestation: Lord Mahā-Viṣṇu, lying upon the Causal Ocean, creating *brahmāṇḍas* like clusters of black pearls suddenly released from their broken strands. Once separated from their maintainer they appear to be cold and dark like dull clay, but when adorning the transcendental figure of the Lord, one can see the lustrous existence of Garbhodakśāyī Viṣṇu deep within their core.

Peering past the glittering effulgence of the *brahma-jyotir*, one can find the form of Lord Gaurāṅga, effulgent with the brilliance of molten gold as He manifests the mood of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and floods the world with His divine love. Of all characteristics, this *ācāryadeva-śiromaṇi* appears to be predominated by this effulgence, but if one looks further, he will be able to find more.

There sit Rādhā-Govinda, seated upon a golden throne like two pendant earrings of tigereye and lapis lazuli. They sit surrounded by Their *sakhās* and *sakhīs*, who are set like gleaming pearls around a golden setting. Their songs of *kṛṣṇa-līlā*, with their punctuation and rhyme, are like the engraving of intricate monograms flecked with bits of diamond upon the face of polished stone. They sit among the groves of Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma with the paths and trails marked by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s footprints, like the veins and spots of polished malachite, set in a box composed of platinum, within which *kṛṣṇa-prema* can always be preserved.

And I wonder who this marvelous personality is, and how he possesses such an unlimited ocean of divine understanding. It is most astounding that this jewel has been in my possession for some time now, but your unlimited facets have not yet begun to reveal themselves to me. I realize that you are simply waiting for my desire to develop as the holy name strips away the unlimited *anarthas* cluttering my heart. You are not anxious to display your opulence, as you know that it is derived only from Kṛṣṇa, just as the *gopīs* knew that the jewels that betrayed the hiding places of their butterpots were not self-effulgent, but were, instead, deriving their illumination from the very bodies of the butter thieves upon which they were worn. Gradually, the absorption in this fascinating gemstone has taken over my senses and robbed me of all other pursuits. I can only think how to serve you, glorify you and introduce others to your divine presence in this world, so that they may be safely conducted to the next.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am begging you to bless me wit an eternal absorption in the at your lotus feet, as the only light in my life. You are the crest jewel of all devotees, dangling from the proud chest of Lord Govinda, challenging the world with your glorious devotion. Let me dedicate my life to enticing the people of the world come bathe in your radiance, which can make the heart more pure than the driven snows of winter. When they see you and hear your words, their hearts will begin to dance, and they will wonder how it was possible to have lived outside of your presence for so long.

For some it is simply a matter of reclaiming a birthright as they take up your service, but as for myself, I know that I am actually like an avaricious thief, who has unexpectedly stumbled upon such a valuable treasure. Please forgive me for not recognizing the rarity of this fortune. If you will have me, I could render some little service at your lotus feet, constantly praying to follow you wherever you go.

*Your worthless servant,*  
Ādi Keśava Swami

## Ātreya Ṛṣi

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I have wasted this life by continuous offenses at your lotus feet. There was not a single moment of your association when I was not offensive. I have even committed so many offenses towards your memories that all your remembrances are vacating me. I now appreciate how Karandhara prabhu felt when he would refuse to have your *darśana*. But once a year, by the mercy of Rādhāballabha prabhu, I am forced to address you. As opportunistic as I am, I am going to beg and pray for the only tangible hope left on this planet for me: Let my mind always meditate and take shelter at the feet of your devotees.

My heart is on fire with material desires. I am wretched and miserable. I have nowhere else to go. I have no other refuge. I beg you with all the sincerity at my command: Let my mind always meditate and take shelter at the lotus feet of your devotees.

Having failed in the association of women, I wasted my time by hoarding gold. Now, all bankrupt and lonely, I lack qualification to associate with my Godbrothers. You are the only person who can qualify me: Let my mind always meditate and take shelter at the lotus feet of your devotees.

My artificial, showbottle humility is even embarrassing to me. I even envy Kāliya. He is so straightforward, and I am so dubious. I fear I am continuously offending my brothers, but I have no other shelter. Now I am praying for your benediction: Let my mind always meditate and take shelter at the lotus feet of your devotees.

A prisoner in this kingdom of my own, my tongue is controlled by lust, my hands tied by envy, and my legs trembling out of fear and illusion. I would rather keep all this and suffer, but the words of wisdom you have left with me keep haunting me. Puzzled and in great anxiety, my only relief is the association of pure devotees. You were my only hope, and now, due to my offenses, you are gone. Now in desperation I cry out to you: Let my mind always meditate and take shelter at the lotus feet of your devotees.

Despite my offenses, your mercy has no bounds. Countless lotus feet of your devotees are interspersed all over this dark and troubled planet: Dayānanda’s cooling white feet; the inviting feet of Bhavānanda; Kīrtanānanda’s lotus feet; Nava’s gentle loving feet; Girirāja’s never-tiring feet, covered by Bombay holy dust; the restless feet of Gopālajī, decorated by those funny toes; Kuṇḍalī’s tiny, light dancing feet; all those never-resting feet of your *sāṅkīrtana* devotees…so much mercy, what a bounty. May the swan of my mind take eternal refuge at the feet of your devotees.

*Your servant*,  
Ātreya Ṛṣi dāsa

## Balavanta dāsa

**Vaiṣṇava Life**

Sometimes, in the midst of the most unlikely places,  
Surrounded by multitudes of unknown faces,  
While carried by car through forests of time  
Or packed in a seat of Flight Four Hundred and Nine,  
My brain bombarded by Māya’s suggestion,  
After so many births, the mind’s filled with impressions.

“May I serve you a drink?” Smiling. “How about a book?”  
Mundane affairs, wherever you look.

But then, out of nowhere, without any cause,  
While reading your books, while taking a pause,  
My mind will lift up to a realm fresh and new,  
To a world pure and clean, to remind me of you.

Than, I’ll remember you pastimes, I’ll think of your teachings,  
I’ll see your vyāsāsana; how bold you were preaching.  
Your only desire: to establish your mission.  
You must pull human life from its fallen condition.

My heart starts to soften, but “Oh, how is this?  
How such a person can experience bliss?”  
Tears willing up; now, what to do?  
I’m filled with emotion just thinking of you.

Of course, no one here is able to tell.  
A devotee learns to conceal his heart so well.  
But one thing I just cannot conceive  
Is how these things can be happening to me.

I’m clearly no different from those untouched by thee-  
An ordinary, common man, that, anyone can see.  
But sometimes, when I look at others, I see spirit soul,  
Each one with the chance of reaching life’s goal.

Contemplating myself, I’m most amazed to find  
A life so transformed in measure, and also in kind.  
What is the cause of this great reformation?  
Who has created this fine situation?

Well, I have no credit, that much is clear.  
The source must by you-the one who is most dear  
To Kṛṣṇa, the ward of all the three worlds.  
You are His servant; you have the control.

You’ve planted the seeds, and that’s no small feat.  
And we are your servants; we fall at your feet.  
But religion’s now vanished with hardly a trace.  
No pious man’s to be found in this fallen place.

If we think for a moment, it’s easy to see  
The entire world’s poised on the brink of catastrophe:  
No war or depression, though that may be, too,  
But losing the chance of surrendering to you.

Still, you’ve created Vaiṣṇavas, their hearts stout and true,  
Their thoughts, words, and deeds dedicated to you.  
But, lo, we are few; the obstacle’s great.  
The world’s filled with lust, with greed, and with hate.

Please give us direction; shine forth your light.  
Make our steps certain; drive out the night.  
Protect your mission, the garden you’ve sown,  
So carefully nurtured, the flowers you’ve grown.

So don’t let us wilt or weeds overcome.  
We’ve got to push forward-there’s word to be done.  
We pray for Vaiṣṇava strength like in days of yore,  
To accomplish the mission you’ve left us here for.

Now let grow a huge tree to cover earth,  
And bring forth its fruit to water this desert.  
The devotional service of Śrī Mahāprabhu  
Will give the world life, afresh and anew.

Only a blessing will bring that happy day.  
Only your direction can show us the way.  
We’re your assistants; we’re ready and willing  
To work for your cause, your visions fulfilling.

Balavanta dāsa

## Bhagavān Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Once, when we were sitting in your room in Bombay, you received a book review in which the professor described you as the incarnation of Vyāsadeva, because it was inconceivable to him how you had written so many deep, philosophical works in such a short time. However, not only have you written down the Vedic literatures exactly in the line of Vyāsa, but by your very appearance in this world you created a veritable sequel to his *Mahābhārata.* How appropriate it is, then, to glorify you on this Vyāsa-pūjā day!

Certainly your appearance was always a part of the master plan of the Supreme Personality of Godhead to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness and diminish the atheistic burden of the earth. Five thousand years ago Kṛṣṇa appeared with His beloved devotees the Pāṇḍavas. From their very youth, Yudhiṣṭhira and his brothers were trained in the military science, which they later expertly used during the Kurukṣetra War. You also were trained from childhood in the use of all the necessary weapons needed to lead the armies of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You practiced *mṛdaṅga* until your hands were full of blood, as Arjuna, in his determination, would shoot his bow even in the dark of night. As the Pāṇḍavas loved Kṛṣṇa from birth, so from your childhood you began to love the Deities of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. You became expert in Sanskrit, and to destroy the ignorance of the world you unleashed the *brahmāstra* of the truth about Kṛṣṇa, which had been hidden for centuries in the *śāstras.*

As Arjuna was stunned by Kṛṣṇa’s determined order to reject his family members and fight on His behalf, so you were shocked to hear the order of your spiritual master, on your very first meeting, to leave everything and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the Western world.

Although it is evident that you were the rightful heir to the mission of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, the Dhṛtarāṣṭras and Duryodhanas of the Gauḍīya Maṭha usurped your father’s kingdom. They made the greatest gamble, thinking that they could be successful by neglecting the orders of their spiritual master. As Duryodhana chose the army and Arjuna chose Kṛṣṇa, so they chose marble and you your Guru Mahārāja. In the end, just as the Kurus, they lost everything, and you gained the entire world and the full blessings of your spiritual master.

To avoid the intrigues of Duryodhana and his brothers, the Pāṇḍavas left Hastināpura only to return later to establish their kingdom at Indraprastha, which far surpassed in excellence the capital of the Kurus. In the same spirit, you created your own glorious ISKCON society, to the envy of many of the Godbrothers, seeing how they had turned Māyāpur into “*Māyā pura.*” Although it was always your pure desire to engage them all in the service of the *saṅkīrtana-yajña,* simply by seeing your success they became more and more disturbed, just as the Kurus became envious of Yudhiṣṭhira’s success in the Rājasūya sacrifice.

By the arrangement of the Lord, the Pāṇḍavas had to leave every thing - family, friends, position and wealth - and enter into forest for so many years. So it was by Kṛṣṇa’s supreme plan that you left your family and shelter in the holy *dhāma* to come to black forest of the Western countries with hardly money enough for one day’s food.

As the Pāṇḍava princes underwent so many hardships - sometimes laying on the cold ground, being attacked by demons and *rākṣasas* and being in the grips of death (sometimes having drunk poisoned water) so you also had to sleep on the cold office in New York, suffer deadly heart attacks, and face the menacing challenges of the atheists and derelicts.

Always your heart was burning with the desire to reclaim the countries of the world under the single flag of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. (You even told us that if we would cooperate with you, you would show us how to conquer the world in eighteen days!) Thus while in the forest of the Western countries you skillfully gathered your faithful armies, patiently pulling out the forest weeds that had overgrown their hearts, and training them to use weapons needed to win the forthcoming battle.

Then, with some of your forces, you again entered Bhārata-varṣa, and in great struggles with Godbrothers, Gosvāmīs and municipal demons, you established your capitals in Māyāpur, Vṛndāvana and Bombay. It was obvious that we had little to do with the victory. You single-handedly fought the battle with Kṛṣṇa driving your chariot.

Just as Arjuna received the knowledge of *Bhagavad-gītā* because he was Kṛṣṇa’s most dear devotee and friend, so due to your intimate relation with Vāsudeva, He empowered you to translate and give the explanations to the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* For all practical purposes, it was as if the *Gītā* had been spoken again after five thousand years, for without your Bhaktivedanta purports, who could have ever understood the message of Kṛṣṇa and Arjuna?

Through the activities of the pure devotees, the mind and plan of the Supreme Personality of Godhead becomes manifest. So it was that through the agency of the Pāṇḍavas the will of the Lord to polarize the theists and the atheists for the ultimate destruction of the demoniac class took place. Now that the world had again become too much afflicted by the burden of the atheists and their leaders, you revealed to us that your ISKCON movement is meant to again polarize the world. We are attentively watching how the new Krurkṣetra War is unfolding to usher in the golden of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Under the influence of Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhira, Kṛṣṇa consciousness was felt all over the world. Now, after five thousand years, the world is again feeling the presence of Kṛṣṇa – from Vṛndāvana to New York to the jungles of Africa, the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement is known. By reestablishing Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the planet you have revealed yourself to be the most powerful of Kṛṣṇa’s *avatāras.* You are visibly the flower on the stem of the Brahma-gauḍīya- sampradāya, and ISKCON is your attractive fragrance being spread everywhere by your desire.

When the time had come, the Pāṇḍavas retired from the management of the kingdom, feeling that things would be secure under the direction of Mahārāja Parīkṣit. In your final days with us in Vṛndāvana you repeatedly asked if things would go on progressively in your absence. However, Parīkṣit Mahārāja instructed us how cautious we must be as leaders, lest we forget ourselves in our exalted positions and foolishly allow Kali-yuga to enter this great movement.

My dear master, as men and celestials in the past heard and relished the wonderful topics of Vyāsadeva’s *Mahābhārata*, so they are now taking great pleasure in the nectarean descriptions of your divine *līlā.* Certainly in the higher planets and the Vaikuṇṭhalokas they are glorifying how you have saved this planet in its darkest hours. Even the demigods are waiting to take birth in your movement and worship you as *jagad-guru*!

In my heart I never feel as though you have left. Rather, your *līlā* is continuing to expand daily as you direct, encourage, and correct us in spreading Lord Caitanya’s movement. As Lord Ananta invisibly maintains all the planets on His hoods, so although not physically manifest before us it is you who are directly maintaining this movement, for certainly we would have been crushed long ago under the great burden.

Although I have no qualification and I am your most fallen servant, I am begging to always assist you in your eternal pastime, wherever you may be. Please never leave me; I am made simply from your merciful glance.

Bhagavān Goswami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

Prabhupāda, my lord and master,  
Over two years have passed since  
The disappearance of Your Divine Grace.

Everyone tells me you’re still with us.  
Most fallen and most unqualified of all,  
I haven’t recovered from the agony of separation.

Visited your home in New Dvārakā,  
Five years ago there I saw you last.

Saw everything except the mṛdaṅga,  
Silently listening to the melody of your bhajana,  
Freshly remembering your kind call,  
“Come on, take this prasāda.”

Followed the dust of your divine footprints  
At Venice Beach at six in the morning.

Saw the serene and calm waves,  
Those lovely sandpipers  
Still following the course of the waves.

Everything looked empty without Your Divine Grace.

Had darśana of your divine garden.

The tree behind the wall was gone,  
The grasses showed only signs of sadness,  
No words came to burn the incense.

There was silence in every direction-  
No one to listen to my worthless report.

Affectionate were your glances,  
Loving were your words,  
Compassionate were your dealings,  
Forceful was your order:

“Broadcast the science of Kṛṣṇa,  
Life comes from life.”

The letter I drafted eight years ago,  
Never had the courage to mail to you.

I was timid and shy.  
However, it contained the essence of my life.

It must be Kṛṣṇa’s mercy;  
Is it necessary to disclose?

On this day of your divine appearance  
Kindly listen to my humble call.

Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda!

Your mercy is all that I am made of,  
You know everything in my heart.

There’s no one like you in the three worlds.

Bless me to carry on your divine message  
Guide me to execute your divine order.

Instruct me to broadcast the message of bhagavat-sevā.

Make me fearless to the molecular men  
Who came out from the primordial soup.

Allow me to carry your victory flag.

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ*  
*paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande*  
*śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*  
(*Madhya* 17.80)

By your divine mercy  
A dumb can speak, a lame can cross over mountains.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Jagad-guru!

*Your eternal, insignificant servant,*  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhavānanda Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The perfection of life is to seek perfection at your lotus feet.

The mercy of the spiritual master is his ability to give shelter, through spiritual instruction, to his disciple and guarantee the delivery of the wretched soul back to the abode of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

The qualification of a disciple is to sincerely serve the lotus feet of the spiritual master, taking his instructions upon his head.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you instructed me in Śrīdhāma Māyāpur in 1977 that these Indian projects were the result of ten years of hard labor. Learning forward on your desk with your right hand supporting your head, looking deep into my eyes, in a soft voice you said, “Please, it is my special request to you, do not let one square inch of these projects deteriorate. Please, do not let one square inch of Māyāpur and Vṛndāvana deteriorate!”

In order to maintain Māyāpur and Vṛndāvana it requires more than washing the temple of sweeping the road. It requires that I surrender deeply to the transcendental *dhāmas* which dwell deep within my heart. It requires constant cleansing of my heart by pure chanting of the holy name and constant meditation on your divine activities and instructions while you were here with us, and your divine activities after your departure. In following those instructions as my very life, I am sure my heart will have all pollution dispelled, and in that perfect state of consciousness I will have your blessed *darśana.*

How unfortunate I now am!

If only I had realized who you were while you were here with me. Deluded by lust and envy I could not hear you even while sitting before you.

Why am I so fallen that in order for me to appreciate your divine position it is necessary for you to leave me again and again? Again and again I fall, and again and again you so tenderly appear to help me.

*Kabe ha’be bolo se dīna āmār.* When, oh, when will that day be mine? When, with an offenseless heart, will I serve Your Divine Grace and again imbibe in that exquisite reciprocation of love and service at your divine lotus feet?

Beating my dead against the rocks, I beg to become your humble and loving servant.

Bhavānanda Goswami

## Brahmānanda Swami

**Remembrance of Things Eternal**

From the very beginning I served you, I saw with my own eyes how you persevered, practically alone, having to do everything yourself. I saw how you cooked your own *capātī,* how you typed your own manuscripts, then edited and even tried for publishing. I saw how you wrote your own letters, and you even were washing your own clothes. Your gave all lectures, and you led all the *kīrtanas.* And you conceived of something called the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and conquered American materialism as a one-man army. You were totally Kṛṣṇa conscious at every moment, and even though you had to care for yourself at the same time, you were able to teach the most wonderful things it the world.

Then you allowed me to do your personal things. I would go out to the store for you. I cut your hair, washed your dishes and even nursed you in sickness. And I helped you to push on the movement. First, I managed the center for you. (You asked me who should be the president. I told you another devotee, but you said no, that I should do it. And that was that.)

You even let me publish your books. I shall never forget the ecstasy when we received the first shipment of *Bhagavad-gītās.* They were delivered right to the door of the storefront one evening at six o’clock, just before the evening *kīrtana.* Afterwards, I stood up to give the customary concluding announcements. But this time, I held in my hands this beautiful purple-colored book and practically cried as I announced that finally your labor of love was published and everyone could take advantage of it. Now the world could read the *Bhagavad-gītā,* as it is, for the first time. You were not in New York but on the West Coast, so I had to mail you’re the three copies, with the satisfaction of knowing you would be pleased even with its abbreviated format.

Then *Teaching of Lord Caitanya* arrived from Japan. I rented a big truck and drove down to the docks. Amidst all the lackadaisical stewards, I myself had to load one ton of Lord Caitanya’s teachings into the truck and drive back to Second Avenue for all the devotees to come down and joyously carry all the volumes upstairs into the temple.

Next, we set up your own press. We were all amateur, boyish Gutenbergs, printing the first books that set forth the world’s most wonderful religion. The ink we used was like your precious blood and the paper your divine body, and the finished book that rolled out of the binder was you. Even recently in Calcutta, I visited your Guru Mahārāja’s temple and noticed how he had built the press as part of the temple, so that the Deity could see it and even hear it working.

I don’t think we can actually fathom just how important the distribution of these books is to you. It is actually the personal order you received from Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī. It is you spiritual mission and your very life. I can remember your sending me to Pakistan. I had no books, and it was so difficult just to be there trying to preach, and finally I had to leave. I went to you in Bombay, and you inquired if I had distributed books there. The fact that I didn’t have any with me because I had to travel by road all the way from Europe and could only carry personal copies didn’t matter. “At least did you sell subscriptions to *Back to Godhead?*” was your question. The purport is that somehow or other, book distribution must go on, even if you don’t have any books and no matter what country you may be in or whatever situation.

The last book I published for you in those early days was your most glorious *Kṛṣṇa* book. You yourself used to sit and read it for pleasure and marvel at how nicely it had been written, almost as if it had been written by someone else. (Later, you let it be known that it had.)

Than you gave me *sannyāsa*, the best kind of spiritual life, and sent me out with nothing, to travel and preach as extensively as possible. But the one thing I had was the remembrance of you, of how it had been in the beginning when you were persevering practically alone. That sweet remembrance has enabled me to endure all sorts of difficulties. That, and my love for you.

And now, here in this rough land of Africa, I am still having to live as you did. I have to look after my own needs, living practically alone. I wash my own clothes and cook my own *capātī* and write my own letters. And, like you, I am introducing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to people who are seeing it for the first time. But they like it. They purchase books, and now I am giving the m beads to chant on, and they also ask questions from what they’ve been reading. I think of you and how we first gathered around you to hear the most sublime message that Lord Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead and that He lives and we can live with Him. You once kindly thanked me for remaining here in this strange culture in a land for from my home. But by your mercy, I don’t find the people strange. It is you who are sending them to me, and it is you who have sent me to them, so in you we are united. We are brothers, myself and the Africans, because you are our all-loving father. You didn’t neglect Africa in your world scheme for then thousand years of Kṛṣṇa conscious culture. And amongst your numerous disciples, many of whom are more sincere and qualified than myself, you didn’t forget me. You’ve kept me by your side as a son, even though I am so fallen and disgraceful. This is your great mercy. So I thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing me to relive “the good old days,” (as you once called them), the best days for me life, the eternal Vṛndāvana days of youth filled with love and intimacy. I listen to the tapes of the old early *kīrtanas*, and I remember you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I remember you because I love you, and tears come to my eyes. I can never become you, but by having to live like you, at least I can remember you. And that is the most important thing.

Brahmānanda Swami

## Gopāla Kṛṣṇa dāsa

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

On this most revered and auspicious day, I beg to offer my most fallen obeisances at the lotus feet of my eternal spiritual master, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktivedanta Goswami Mahārāja, founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness and the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust. Your lotus feet offer all benedictions and complete shelter to all who follow your instructions. Though you are not present in your personal, or *vapuḥ*, form, you will be eternally present in your instruction, or *vāṇī*, form. A genuine disciple can associate with his spiritual master by having faith in his *vāṇī.*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are eternally perfect, *nitya-siddha.* Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu has predicted that the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* will spread to every town and village of the world. Your appearance on this planet in 1896 was just to make that prediction come true. You cam to illuminate this dark world with transcendental knowledge, and you were very successful in your mission. In a brief span of twelve years, you wrote nearly seventy books and opened over one hundred and fifty centers all over the world. No saint in recent history has performed anything comparable to this. Prior to the appearance of your books, very few knew the purpose of the *Vedas*. The *Vedas* give us knowledge about getting out of the material world by surrendering to Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. The purpose of Vedic study is to understand Kṛṣṇa – *vedaiś ca sarvair aham eva vedyo vedānta-kṛd veda-vid eva cāham.* Commentaries by mental speculators and bogus gurus had misled the already confused masses. Though in nearly every page of the *Gītā* the words *mām* (“unto Me”) and *Śrī Bhagavān uvāca* are used, still these demoniac commentators dare say that the Supreme is impersonal. These commentaries were available not only in the West but also in India. Several *svāmīs* came from India to present the *Bhagavad-gītā* to the West, but none were successful. Arjuna became the recipient of this great science of *Bhagavad-gītā* owing to his becoming a confidential devotee of the Lord. In order to get the spiritual benefit from hearing the *Gītā* or *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* one must hear from a bona fide spiritual authority, not a word juggler or a professional reciter. By presenting word-by-word meaning, translation, and elaborate purports, you have established that the Supreme is a sentient being, and His original name is Kṛṣṇa. The process for understanding Him is by executing devotional service by following the devotional manuals – your books. In executing devotional service, it is essential that one hears properly from a bona fide spiritual master. If one dears properly, then he can preach properly. You were always serious about preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness. When you first started attending Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta’s lectures, you would sit through and listen attentively, even if you could not understand, while others left.

Spiritual science is absolute because it is coming from the perfect person and is explained by the perfect representative. Material science, on the other hand, is imperfect and subject to regular contradictions. You were the perfect spiritual doctor for this century. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the *jagad-guru.* You were like the desire tree that can produce unlimited nectar required for Kṛṣṇa’s service. You were able to engage everyone you met in Kṛṣṇa’s service. Impersonalists, atheists, scientists were all convinced by you that there is a Supreme Lord who is a person and who can be understood by executing devotional service. You destroyed the teachings of the Māyāvādīs single-handedly and converted thousands of crowlike men into swanlike. The entire humanity is eternally indebted to you for setting up the worldwide structure of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

People are thinking they can enjoy this material world very nicely. This idea is illusion, as there is no enjoyment in the material world. Whether one is in the position of a demigod, president or pauper, no one has been victorious over the struggle of birth, death and disease. At any moment one can be shot down. The foolish materialists are cleaning the cage instead of feeding the bird inside. We are neglecting the real comfort and are identifying the material cage with ourselves. The captive soul within the cage is being neglected. No one is trying to feed the soul. The nature of the soul is spiritual and not material. Therefore, the living entities in spiritual activities, you formed the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. The purpose of this Society is to establish *dharma*, the principles of religion. You wanted this movement to go down in history as the movement that has saved the world. As this was your desire, it is the responsibility of your disciples, granddisciples and all the future disciples of this great movement to sacrifice everything to spread your teachings by distributing your books and *prasāda.* You showed by your personal example that a devotee should not be afraid of struggling for Kṛṣṇa. Your life was full of struggles. You struggled in India to preach, then you had to struggle to print and distribute your books all alone in India. You struggled to get a passage to come to the West, and after you came to the West, you struggled all alone, at the age of seventy, to open the first Kṛṣṇa conscious temple. In 1976, wen your health was poor, your disciples suggested that you please take rest in Gītā-nāgarī and stop traveling. You refused the request by replying, “Don’t take away my privilege to struggle for Kṛṣṇa.” This statement of yours is very important for all present and future devotees of this movement.

In attempting to increase book distribution and in expanding the preaching, devotees are constantly facing obstacles. Kṛṣṇa is forcing us to struggle to serve Him. Kṛṣṇa is always testing to see whether we want *māyā* or Him; and if we consistently pass our tests, then Kṛṣṇa will recognize our sincerity and give the necessary facilities. We can resist *māyā’s* temptations only by remembering your divine instructions. You wanted all the members of ISKCON to work together to push on this movement, which is the need of the hour. On this auspicious day, I am praying for your causeless mercy so that I may become a tiny instrument in spreading your teachings to the suffering humanity. May I always live by following your divine instructions and never deviate from them, even for a moment.

*Your useless servant,*  
Gopāla Kṛṣṇa dāsa

## Haṁsadūta Swami

My lord and master, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
At last you’ve crushed my iron heart  
With the mighty branch of the Caitanya tree,  
On which hangs the fruit called GBC.

I worship your through the GBC.  
The fruit of the Caitanya tree.  
Twenty-three, and all are one.  
Excuse my candle held to the sun.

Śrīla Bhagavān Mahārāja,  
Devotees, books, and castles large,  
Young, healthy, wealthy, pure and wise –  
In you there is nothing to criticize.

Śrīla Kīrtanānanda Bhaktipāda,  
The first and best of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s,  
Built a palace in USA.

It speaks for itself, what can I say?

Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Harikeśa Swami,  
Wizard, musician, preacher, geni,  
Translating in Russian, Hungarian, and Arabic –  
A daring preacher, using every trick.

Śrīla Rāmeśvara Caitanyapāda,  
Next to you I am only a clod.

Prabhupāda said none of us have brains,  
But this boy has a little intelligence.

Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Gurudeva,  
The book distribution road you paved.

In every way an ideal guru.  
I want to go to Chine with you.

Śrīla Hṛdayānanda Ācāryadeva,  
I think of you always from Berkeley days.

A scholar, preacher, humorous wit,  
Now you are translation Sanskrit.

Śrīla Bhavānanda Goswami,  
Dashing, handsome and carefree.

Rubies, diamonds, rings and pearls  
Just to attract fools to spiritual worlds.

Śrīla Jayapatāka Ācāryapāda,  
Very determined, very hard.

You taught us parliamentary rule  
To help establish the Bhāgavata school.

Śrīla Jayatīrtha Goswami,  
The GBC has set you free.  
Now all eleven are in saffron,  
So preaching will go on and on.

Śrīla Satsvarūpa Goswami,  
Our literary expert personality.

In line with guru, śāstra and sādhu,  
I think of you also as my guru.  
Śrīmān Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara,  
ISKCON scientific star,  
Expose and challenge scientific chatter.

Life comes from life, not life from matter.

Śrīmān Ādi Keśava Swami,  
In New York, instead of under a tree.

Although young, boyish, frail and thin,  
Preaching like a lion in the capital of sin.

Śrīmān Prabhupāda-kṛpa Goswami,  
Kṛpā-siddha of Prabhupāda Swami.

Devoted servant of Oṁ Viṣṇupāda,  
You are one of Prabhupāda’s most stalwart.

Śrīmān Jagajīvana Goswami,  
Opened Argentina, Peru, Bolivia and Chile.

Many devotees made, in dangerous places.  
You carry all of Prabhupāda’s graces.

Śrīmān Pañcadraviḍa Swami,  
We preached to you in Germany.

You preached in Thailand, Hong Kong and India.  
Now you are delivering South America.

Śrīmān Brahmānanda Swami,  
ISKCON’s African missionary.

Why has Prabhupāda sent you there?  
You can do what others do not dare.

Education Minister, Śrīmān Jagadīśa,  
Establishing Guru-kula just to teach  
Kṛṣṇa-kathā, ABC, and Sanskrit, too.

Practically you are the children’s guru.

Śrīmān Ātreya Ṛṣi  
Preaching in Parsi.

The Shah toppled from the throne,  
Still your Iran preaching goes on.

One of Prabhupāda’s oldest is Rūpānuga,  
A brilliant light in Kali-yuga.

You joined at Second Avenue.  
We did, too, being attracted by you.

Śrīmān Balavanta dāsa adhikārī,  
Serving Kṛṣṇa with a law career,  
Ran for mayor of Atlanta,  
Political Hare Kṛṣṇa chanter.

Śrīmān Gopāla Kṛṣṇa prabhu,  
We preached in Montreal to you.

From there you’ve gone a long, long way –  
Indian BBT, GBC of Canada and Bombay.

Śrīmān Vāsudeva dāsa adhikārī,  
Delivering the island of Fiji,  
Built a Kṛṣṇa-Kāliya temple.

For us, your activities are on example.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, supreme guru,  
Worshiping your devotees is worshiping you.

Each devotee one of your aspects,  
You are the GBC in all respects.

Haṁsadūta Swami

## Harikeśa Swami

I offer my most respectful obeisances in the divine dust of the lotus feet of my most exalted spiritual master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. I offer these obeisances carefully, fully considering that which I have realized about Śrīla Prabhupāda and my relationship with him over the past few months. I meditate on his glories from the point of view of my practical realization, and enunciate them according to my limited abilities. Simultaneously hoping that this offering will please Śrīla Prabhupāda and further illumine the gravity and depth of his vision and activities, I humbly present a Vyāsa-pūjā offering to him.

My Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Most people have a hard time admitting that there is something which they don’t understand. Usually they take great pride in having figured out what they feel is their relevant piece of the universe. Any intruder into this sacred territory is immediately confronted with fear and indignation and accosted by the ever-ready defense forces of the psyche. Although I am such a normal fool, when I analyze my understanding of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I must readily admit that there is so much I still do not understand, neither am I very confident that I will obtain a complete understanding in the near future. I therefore feel unable to properly praise you, who are so unfathomable to the mundane mind.

My generally overblown opinion of myself is quickly deflated at the time of my writing for your yearly Vyāsa-pūjā book. As soon as I sit at the typewriter I find myself bland, frighteningly empty, suddenly devoid of inspiration, sweating inwardly and silently voicing doubts about my ability to properly praise you. I want you to be pleased with me, yet although incompetent, I must publicly offer you these prayers. I once again realize how useless I am and how bankrupt is my intelligence. The coffers of my brain are only filled when you fill them, yet asking you to give me the intelligence to offer proper praise at your feet seems like some kind of cheating; however, I find myself without any other recourse than to accept such a second-class methodology.

Gradually I begin to type on the page, and the clean white space becomes littered with valueless words, which although accepted by my pride as glittering gold are in reality simply a cheap imitation. Page after page is ripped out of the machine and torn to shreds in quick motions of undisguised disgust. Why can’t I properly praise you when I have to? What is the reason for this sudden impotence? I can only guess it is because my words are not connected with my innermost realizations of Your Lordship. Suddenly I realize that I have been cheating. I have been struggling to copy that which I have heard before, stereotyped phrases of antiquity, true and certainly purely spiritual, but not my own realization of you, my spiritual master, and thus I am unable to express myself in those terms without sounding dry and stale. Pricked by this realization, a thought hits me like a thunderbolt, and it is feverishly written on the pare. As the first thought came, a second comes just as mysteriously, followed by a shower of thoughts flowing onto the page, which soon becomes a stream, complete with a cohesive theme delineating the literary borders of this glorification.

Generally I have no problem verbally praising you, for such words of glorification flow naturally from one’s mouth like showers of water cascading down a mountain. After a great victory over the demons, or the ever-increasing attacks of *māyā,* or even after doing something which is obviously pleasing to you, praise of your wondrous personality spontaneously erupts and surges forth without hindrance from and quarter. All devotional obstacles stand powerless before your memory, for this process of praise is as natural as one’s very breathing. This eruption of realization reveals descriptions of Your Lordship not even understood by their speaker a moment before. As he speaks, his realization increases, causing further expansions of your glories to manifest, leaving the hearers awestruck.

Unfortunately, one cannot artificially cause such flashes of inspiration to manifest (spontaneity comes under no one’s control), thus writing your Vyāsa-pūjā offering remains the most difficult engagement of the year. repeating simply the standardized praises found often in the *śāstras* may make the job easier of one saw it as a tedious task, but such canned praise is no more pleasing than the canned laughter on the TV. Avoiding such institutionalized, easy praises, one must search within himself, into the recesses of his heart, and offer the deepest and most profound words available.

Once you wanted to tour the holy places of South India by jeep, and thus, trying to assist you, I ventured to look in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* for the itinerary of the Lord, so we could duplicate it. With a sour look you admonished me, “Following in the footsteps does not mean mechanical imitation. I do not have to follow the same tour as Lord Caitanya. We will go another way.” Another lesson learned in the school of hard knocks. *Sādhu-mārgaṇa-gamana* doesn’t mean imitating the same activities as the great souls, rather it means accepting their mission and goals and striving with whatever God-given means are available, with all the intelligence and determination at one’s command to accomplish them.

Again, you would chide all of us Americans disciples, “What is the use of your being Americans if you don’t do something wonderful for Kṛṣṇa?” Had we forgotten our tradition of manifest destiny? Did we think that we could get away with simply being ordinary devotees, quietly chanting in some secluded place? How long would we have lasted doing that anyway? You were constantly pushing us to take the credit of accomplishing this great feat of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world. In one letter to a GBC man you said, “Kṛṣṇa has already arranged the plan for this movement to change the face of this world. It is already predicted that this movement will spread to every town and village. Just as Kṛṣṇa told Arjuna on the Battlefield of Kurukṣetra, *mayaivaite nihatāḥ pūrvam eva nimitta-mātraṁ bhava savyasācin:* it is already accomplished by the Lord’s supreme potency and desire. Simply act as His instrument and take the credit for the victory. Success will be there, it is up to you to take charge of the mission and take the credit. If you do not, someone else will, so please take this mission seriously.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are an expert cowherdsman who knows how to most skillfully use the rod to prod us onwards by poking us in our false egos. You would challenge me, stabbing my intellect: “…big, big brain, can’t even make a *capātī*…,” “always learning, learning, learning, when are you ever going to do something?” phrases which ripped into the soft, cushy layers of the false ego and exposed the flabby internal underflesh of a lazy man – a pitiful sight. You would poke with *sūtras*, pushing me further and further…

I used to think… it seems intolerable – how will I ever survive this attack on my poor ego? What can I say? It is a merciless massacre. I cannot defend myself, and he knows it – yet the shock is increasing more and, the challenge is becoming greater and greater. He is not satisfied that I am simply tolerating being pushed beyond my limits, he is pushing me on and on…

I felt like Dhruva Mahārāja being pushed by the insults of his stepmother – yet weren’t these insults ultimately the cause of his success? Wasn’t this in actuality the supreme benefit of his life? Since you were simultaneously supplying me with painful challenge and the means to accomplish it, aren’t you both my mother and stepmother? And since you placed me on your lap and set me up for being slapped in the face with these seemingly harsh words, aren’t you my father as well? Thus you are everything to me – that which has created what is good within me and destroyed all that is bad.

Like a father too irate with his naughty son to consider the merciful pleas of the child’s mother, you have pulled me from under the dubious cover of the shawls and skirts of my false ego (a wretched woman constantly trying to appease and please me with her sweet words of false praise and encouragement). Reaching into my heart you directly took hold of the hard knots of material attachments and illusions, and twisting them as a dentist pulling teeth, you uprooted them in a swift motion. It was shocking – yes; and painful also. Yet ultimately, like the removal of a bad tooth, it was most beneficial.

You are the most expert in engaging your disciples in the devotional service of the Lord. (The spiritual master has unlimited intelligence to engage his disciples.) You can enter within their hearts and immediately understand their strengths and weaknesses. Proceeding to clear the unwonted weeds in their in their internal gardens, you know exactly how to encourage them to bring out their hidden (nay, stealthily stashed and secreted) potentials. You clean away the dirt, nicely arrange the garden, and engage the soul in expanding and beautifying the area more and more, to make a perfect sitting place for the Lord to enjoy. I am most grateful that Your Lordship can see the hidden potential of everyone and that you expose that potential for all to see.

As you are so capable of seeing the hidden potentials of your disciples, I want to be capable to offer you that potential as you wish to use it. During times of offering praise at your lotus feet, I wish speak words wet with the juice of *bhakti-rasa*, for dry words dutifully falling out of the mouth like so many crackers falling from a box will never please you, even if they are packaged in the most modern and sophisticated terms. If I can succeed in actually offering you appropriate words of praise, then I may increase your bliss, for these words can be made only of a transcendental realization of the importance of your mission and your means of attaining it.

My deepest desire is to be factually able to answer the last question you ever asked me, “Now do you understand why I was doing what I did? Now do you understand the reason for the chastisements? You thought that I was kicking you away, but actually I could not stand to see you rotting behind the typewriter all day, typing my tapes. Now do you understand? Now do you understand?” Lamely I tried putting forward the thesis that I did understand, but we both knew perfectly well that I didn’t. You were stroking my head, and I felt embarrassed that you felt that it was necessary to show me your intentions in such an open way, for I did not doubt them. Out of intense, or desire to demonstrate my confidence, I held your hand, preventing you from touching me, trying foolishly to communicate my feeling that everything was all right – you would get better and go on writing your books. I was thinking, “It’s all right, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I don’t understand, but all I know is that I love you eternally, and whatever you do with me is completely perfect. I fully accept all that you have said and done and will try to manifest that which you wish me to do.” Because you are infinitely wise, you did not accept my feeble protestations, and you seemed frustrated that I held your arm. You wanted to take your hand away and in spite of my objections lovingly stroke my dead again – but I thought that I had to be bold to show you that I was firmly situated in your loving service, I was your man eternally and thus I didn’t need to hear your tearful explanations to keep me under your wing. What a blockhead am I, a dummkopf, *holzkopf*, fool greater than a thousand fools, a total stone – I beat myself again and again. Why did I hold you back? Have I no love, no understanding? Oh, how lamentable, and because of this foolishness you knew I didn’t understand, and again you spoke the same words, only this time looking more anxious than before, the words trailing off in volume, almost hopelessly, “Now do you understand, now do you understand?”

I didn’t understand it then, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It was something too massive for me to understand with only a block on the shoulders. Still now I cannot truthfully say I understand in full, yet I can honestly say that I have begun to have a most exciting glimpse of what you want of this great ISKCON and what exact role you would have me fill within it. When I have fully understood, I wish to fully praise you with my words and deeds. At that time I will offer my full realization at your lotus feet. With all humility at my command I will tearfully fall on the floor before you and say in great ecstasy, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, I understand; I fully understand.” I think then I willfully know the import of your mission here on this planet and will then be able to glorify you in words fully befitting your divine position as the most perfect representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hṛdayānanda Goswami

1\. O most beloved spiritual master, again you have come to me when I was fallen, in the darkness of ignorance, and lit your transcendental torch in the cavern of my heart. For this, my eternal debt increases a millionfold, and I am choked up and speechless.

2\. foolish neophyte cries to see Kṛṣṇa when the beautiful Deity stands before him as Kṛṣṇa Himself, ready to take service. Puffed up with false prestige, we neglect the Deity, which is not different from Kṛṣṇa Himself, until you force us to our knees and teach us the service mood. This is your duty as *guru* – to teach us to serve the Deity.

3\. Everything is your property, because you are the dearmost of Kṛṣṇa. My very body, mind, and life breath are your insignificant property. What to speak of other things that I may dream are mine? Beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, my life and soul are insignificant dust particles in your lotus hand. What to speak of my so-called zone and disciples?

4\. Due to the darkness of ignorance, we imagine some imperfection in the external form, the speech, of the desire of the perfect spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda. This is our madness, for which blazing thunderbolts will shatter our heart and torture our mind. Beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, perfection is what you are, what you speak, and what you desire and manifest. Take pity on me, a conditioned soul, and grant me the boon of again worshiping you.

5\. In your translations and purports of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* you have clearly revealed the spiritual world. You have not indirectly described something, but you have actually opened a wide doorway into the eternal pastimes of the soul with the Supreme Soul, Kṛṣṇa. All of the pastimes of the Lord and His abode and associates are manifest in the pages of your books. We are stunned by your achievement, and with tears in our eyes we offer our heartfelt thanks for your books, such as *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

6\. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* you have revealed the mood of a Vaiṣṇava. All of the future activities of ISKCON will arise from the seed pastimes of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, which you have revealed. You are not teaching a temporary, mundane religious system. You are sticking always on the eternal platform. Therefore, we can perfectly understand how to serve Kṛṣṇa during our present life and how to love and serve each other, and ultimately how to love and serve you. All this can be completely understood in the pages of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*.

7\. There can be no compromise for one who wants to follow you and go back to home, back to Godhead. You have repeatedly stated that we must give up all forms of sense gratification and surrender fully to Kṛṣṇa. O angel of mercy, how can we thank you for coming so heroically into the courtyard of our heart and boldly exposing the great thief and enemy which wickedly hid among us in the form of our own mind? Now help us to break the teeth of this enemy and enslave him, dragging him, bound up, to the royal assembly of devotional service.

8\. O most beloved and revered Śrīla Prabhupāda, surely we have pleased you in some tiny way by forming a GBC body and working cooperatively, as you so fervently desired. If so, we beg you to cast your glance of mercy continually over us. Our only desire is to serve you life after life. Let us go always where you go and serve you in any capacity. Service to your lotus feet is everything for us, and we eternally desire to offer our humble worship to Kṛṣṇa through your lotus feet.

*Your most insignificant disciple who begs for your mercy,*  
Hṛdayānanda Goswami

## Jagadīśa dāsa

**Savior of the World**

What the soul is was not known in the world.  
We were all struggling in darkest abyss.  
No one could know which direction to take,  
No peace of mind, and without happiness.

Then you delivered the knowledge of the soul,  
Sparking remembrance of eternal life,  
Service to Kṛṣṇa our natural role,  
Making the real revolution ignite.

Now, understanding the real common ground,  
We can begin to constructively work  
Under your guidance and by your desire,  
Leading humanity out of the mire.

“Kingdom of God on the earth,” you have said,  
Can be achieved if we purely proclaim  
Gaurāṅga’s teachings to all whom we meet.  
No one restricted from chanting His names.

Nations united by Prabhupāda’s grace,  
Worshiping Kṛṣṇa in His rightful place.  
Many will go back to Godhead for sure!  
Prabhupāda! You are the lord of my life!

Jagadīśa dāsa

## Jagajīvana Goswami

Glorious Vaiṣṇavas, Ācāryas, beloved Godbrothers and friends,

Today we are called upon to glorify he who cannot be glorified. The Lord’s nature and His manifestations within this material world are all of an unlimited, absolute nature. The pastimes of the Lord occurring in one day are impossible to be described and glorified, even by the Lord Himself. During Śrīla Prabhupāda’s merciful presence in this material world, every thought and plan, idea and dream were manifest pastimes of the supreme preaching potency. This was the manifest potency of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the flawless gem of the spiritual nature. Kṛṣṇa says, *daścin me priya-kṛttamaḥ* (Bg. 18.69). And your are kṛṣṇa-preṣṭha, so dear to Kṛṣṇa. It is the gift of the most merciful spiritual entities that they preach when they have personally no need to preach – that they descend even though they be situated in their eternal, spiritual, blissful relationship with Kṛṣṇa. This is confirmed (*na śocati na kāṅakṣati, prāyena munayo rājan, guṇānukathane hareḥ*). No desire to come to hell! The material world means hell. Devoid of Kṛṣṇa consciousness – hellish existence. Understand this mercy of the savior of our heart; see the divine and wondrous progressive liberation plan for the whole human society unfold before your very eyes. All the mystic opulences of the living entities could not mount such an intricate arrangement for ultimate salvation. We, your servants, are enmeshed in this plan, in the fire, in the whorl of the vortex of that plan – and we cannot even see it yet! But we know that when the smoke clears, the Vaiṣṇava historians will set yet another chapter to *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.* And for you, my dear and beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, they will sing your praise and raise on high your wondrous mercy!

Who is knowing Kṛṣṇa? I only know Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy, and even then – what an unfortunate wretch I am – I have no capacity to appreciate it.

*Your lowly servant,*  
Jagajīvana Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

When Your Divine Grace first saw me, you asked me to accept *prasāda* at your house. You stuffed me until I thought I would burst at the seam. You engaged me in Kṛṣṇa’s service, washing the temple floor and cleansing pots and pans. What a wonderful time it was to have your *darśana* and serve your feet as your menial servant.

Your Divine Grace asked me to be president of a temple, although I was lowest of all. And since then you have engaged me in various services. You ordered me to India, with your association so dear. Your Divine Grace was anxious that the Māyāpur Candrodaya Mandira floor should be clean and pure.

Now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you’ve appointed me to initiate, too. People worship me as a guru. I can only remember that I once washed your floor, and your servant I must always remain. Don’t let me forget you in illusion or pride. I am your menial servant. Let that be my singular honor and glory. You made me; you can break me. I am simply your slave. Your chastisement is your mercy – to keep me in line. Śrīla Prabhupāda, help me to please you and fulfill your desires in my time.

Your Divine Grace has taught us to be a fool.  
A fool before the spiritual master is the wisest before all.  
Such a fool is not proud; he acts as your tool.  
Let me always stand a fool before you – never to fall.

Your Divine Grace has taught us to be like a rose.  
The devotee whose heart is like a rose soft and pure  
By crying for souls fallen, divine potency grows.  
Seeing good in the bad, he acts seeing Kṛṣṇa’s the doer.

Your Divine Grace has taught us to be like a lion.  
Principles against śāstra and guru must be smashed without fear.  
Like a lion, armed with your mercy, he devours the doer of deviation,  
Steady, too, in the storm, remembering you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are here.

Your Divine Grace has taught us to be like the grass  
Bending when stepped on, a grass blade soon stands again.  
A devotee, bending under chastisement’s foot, lets it pass,  
Springs back, surrendered in service, taking the nectar of your mercy rain.

Your Divine Grace has taught us to be like tree.  
Tolerate all difficulties except offenses against you.  
As a tree whose branches are cut accepts silently,  
Your devotee, patient amidst all the tests, anxious to please only you.

Your Divine Grace has taught us to be like a swan –  
To separate the real from the imitations.  
Taking milk from the water is possible for a swan.  
You are the spiritual swan eternally, giving us inspirations.

Your Divine Grace has given us Nārada’s course:  
To remain but a few days to preach, then to go,  
To chant and to serve without fattening to purse –  
A chance to give every soul your message to know.

Your Divine Grace has opened our eyes with the torchlight.  
I shut my eyes tight, but you forced me to see.  
You are my friend, my master, me ever-guiding light.  
At your lotus feet I beg to serve you purely.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Jayapatāka Swami

## Jayatīrtha Goswami

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my repeated prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. On this most auspicious of days, I am submitting myself to you for punishment and chastisement for all my sins and offenses and am praying for severe treatment so that I may be corrected and in the upcoming years render some meaningful service to your supremely glorious movement.

In the company of your intelligent disciple Śrīmān Kuśakratha dāsa brahmacārī, I made some small contribution to the composition of the following aṣṭakam, which I now wish to offer to you, praying that your will find it appropriate.

*śrī-śrī-navadvīpa-para-pradīpaḥ*  
*sandīpyamānaḥ satato bhuvīha*  
*cetas-tamo hanti hi yasya yatnāt*  
*taṁ kīrtayāmaḥ prabhupādadevam (1)*

*śrī-śrī-navadvīpa-para-pradīpaḥ* – Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the transcendental lamp of Navadvīpa; *sandīpyamānaḥ* – brilliant blazing; *satataḥ* – eternal; *bhuvi* – in the world; *iha* – here; *cetaḥ* – of the heart; *tamaḥ* – the darkness of ignorance; *hanti* – indeed; *yasya* – of whom; *yatnāt* – because of the endeavor; *tam* – him; *kīrtayāmaḥ* – let us glorify; *prabhupādadevam* – His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

**Let us continuously glorify His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, by whose efforts the hearts of all living entities, which were pervaded by darkness, are becoming illumined with the brilliant and everlasting lamp of the teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, whose eternal abode is the holy dhāma of Navadvīpa.**

Note: This poem is written in the meter *upajāti*, the same as *Śrī Gurvaṣṭakam* of Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī.

*śrī-bhaktisiddhānta-pracoditaḥ śrī-*  
*vyāsādya-kośaṁ kuśalaṁ kṛpāluḥ*  
*san mleccha-vāg-deśa-caye ’dadhad yas*  
*taṁ kīrtayāmaḥ prabhupādadevam (2)*

*śrī-bhaktisiddhānta* – by Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura Prabhupāda; *pracoditaḥ* – ordered; *śrī-vyāsa-ādya* – of Vyāsa and others; *kośam* – the treasure house (of scriptures); *kuśalam* – auspicious; *kṛpāluḥ* – merciful; *san* – being so; *mleccha-vāk-deśa-caye* – in the Western countries where English and other languages are spoken; *adadhat* – placed; *yaḥ* – who; *tam* – him; *kīrtayāmaḥ* – let us glorify; *prabhupādadevam* - His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

**Taking the order of his spiritual master, Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, as his life, he showed his causeless mercy on the lowest born creatures, the inhabitants of the Western countries, by translating, publishing and distributing the auspicious treasure house of devotional literatures written by Śrīla Vyāsadeva, Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja and others. Let us, therefore, continuously glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda.**

*ācchadyate yat-para-tarka-dṛśya-*  
*govinda-mūrty-ujjvala-bhāskareṇa*  
*advaiti-khadyota-kulaṁ jagatyāṁ*  
*taṁ kīrtayāmaḥ prabhupādadevam (3)*

*ācchadyate* – is overshadowed; *yat* – of whom; *para* – transcendental; *tarka* – because of the logic; *dṛśya* – visible; *govinda* – of Lord Govinda; *mūrti* – the transcendental form; *ujjvala* – splendid; *bhāskareṇa* – by the sun; *advaiti* – impersonalist philosophers; *khadyota* – glowworms; *kulam* – multitude; *jagatyām* – in the world; *tam* – him; *kīrtayāmaḥ* – let us glorify; *prabhupādadevam* - His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

**The last snare of nescience had been set in place by the Māyāvādī “gurus” and “svāmīs,” who appeared like so many glowworms. When, however, the brilliant sun of Lord Govinda’s transcendental form began to illuminate the world, having been established, through the expert logic of Śrīla Prabhupāda, as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the glowworms were naturally eclipsed . Let us, therefore, continuously offer praise and adoration to His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda.**

*yasyaiva yatnena purāṇa-rāja-*  
*gaṅgāmalā keśava-keli-haṁsā*  
*sat-stotra-ratnā vahatīha loke*  
*taṁ kīrtayāmaḥ prabhupādadevam (4)*

*yasya* – of whom; *eva* – indeed; *yatnena* – by the endeavor; *purāṇa-rāja* – the king of the Purāṇas (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*); *gaṅgā* – the Ganges river; *amalā* – pure and crystal clear; *keśava* – of Lord Keśava; *keli* – the pastimes; *haṁsā* – with swans; *sat* – of the pure devotees; *stotra* – of prayers; *ratnā* – with jewels; *vahati* – flows; *iha* – here; *loke* – in this world; *tam* – him; *kīrtayāmaḥ* – let us glorify; *prabhupādadevam* - His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

**Let us glorify His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Because of his efforts, the pure and crystal-clear Ganges river of Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, the king of the Purāṇas, is now flowing all over the world. The pebbles in that river are the jewels which are the prayers of the pure devotees, and playing within its waters are the swans which are the pastimes of Lord Keśava.**

*śrī-rādhikā-kṛṣṇa-padāravinde*  
*sahasra-hṛtsu śata-mandireṣu*  
*aropayad yo madhure pṛthivyāṁ*  
*taṁ kīrtayāmaḥ prabhupādadevam (5)*

*śrī-rādhikā-kṛṣṇa* – of Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa; *pada* – feet; *aravinde* – two lotus flowers; *sahasra* – thousands; *hṛtsu* – in hearts; *śata* – a hundred; *mandireṣu* – in temples; *aropayat* – planted; *yaḥ* – who; *madhure* – charming; *pṛthivyām* – in this world; *tam* – him; *kīrtayāmaḥ* – let us glorify; *prabhupādadevam* - His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

**Let us glorify His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who planted the charming lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa in hundreds of temples and thousands of hearts in this world.**

*kauśalyadair yasya sahasra-śāstra-*  
*śastrātivarṣaiḥ pratihanyate hi*  
*caṇḍā kaler moha-camūr haṭhena*  
*taṁ kīrtayāmaḥ prabhupādadevam (6)*

*kauśalyadaiḥ* – granting auspiciousness; *yasya* – of whom; *sahasra* – thousands; *śāstra* – of books; *śastra* – of weapons; *ati* – great; *varṣaiḥ* – with showers; *pratihanyate* – is destroyed; *hi* – certainly; *caṇḍā* – fierce; *kaleḥ* – of the age of Kali; *moha* – of the illusion; *camūḥ* – the army; *haṭhena* – violently; *tam* – him; *kīrtayāmaḥ* – let us glorify; *prabhupādadevam* - A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

**Seeing the fierce army of illusions put forward by the forces of Kali-yuga, Śrīla Prabhupāda has released an unlimited shower of powerful arrows in the form of his books. In this way, truth is emerging as the victor. Let us, therefore, double and redouble our glorification of Śrīla Prabhupāda.**

*nānā-lasad-gaura-sudhā-prakāśa-*  
*phalaḥ kṣitau sthāpita eva yena*  
*kṛṣṇānucintā-jana-saṅgha-vṛkṣas*  
*taṁ kīrtayāmaḥ prabhupādadevam (7)*

*nānā* – various; *lasat* – splendid; *gaura* – of Lord Caitanya; *sudhā* – the nectar; *prakāśa* – manifestation; *phalaḥ* – with fruits; *kṣitau* – on the earth; *sthāpitaḥ* – established; *eva* – certainly; *yena* – by whom; *kṛṣṇa-anucintā-jana-saṅgha* – the International Society for Krishna Consciousness; *vṛkṣaḥ* – the transcendental desire tree; *tam* – him; *kīrtayāmaḥ* – let us glorify; *prabhupādadevam* – His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda

**Let us glorify His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. The desire tree of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, whose many splendid fruits are different methods of distributing Lord Caitanya’s nectarean message, has been planted by him upon this earth.**

*yo nityadā jīvati daivikīṣu*  
*nijāsu śikṣāsu tu sānugāmī*  
*kṛpāmbudhir nanda-sutāśritātmā*  
*taṁ kīrtayāmaḥ prabhupādadevam (8)*

*yaḥ* – who; *nityadā* – eternally; *jīvati* – lives; *daivikīṣu* – divine; *nijāsu* – own; *śikṣāsu* – in the instructions; *tu* – indeed; *sa* – accompanied by; *anugāmī* – his sincere followers; *kṛpā* – of mercy; *ambudhiḥ* – like an ocean; *nanda* – of Nanda Mahārāja; *suta* – of the transcendental son; *āśrita* – completely taken shelter; *ātmā* – with his body, mind, words and self; *tam* – him; *kīrtayāmaḥ* – let us glorify; *prabhupādadevam* - His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

**Let us forever glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda, the unlimited ocean of mercy. With his body, mind, words and self, he has taken complete shelter of the son of Nanda Mahārāja, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda lives forever by his divine instructions, and his followers live with him.**

*ye vā idaṁ śrī-prabhupādadeva-*  
*kīrty-aṣṭakaṁ suṣṭhu paṭhanti nityam*  
*gaurodayācārya-vicitra-śaktyā*  
*te kṛṣṇa-vārtāṁ pravadanti bhūmyām (9)*

*ye* – those who; *vai* – certainly; *idam* – this; *śrī-prabhupādadeva-kīrti* – glorifying His Divine Grace A.C.
